### [Algorithmic Reflectivity in Large Language Models: Methodological Study of Linguistic Structure in Mentalization](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e90750/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-10-20` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e90750/`

Background: Mentalization is a core human capacity involving interpretation of one’s own and others’ behavior in terms of underlying mental states. Within the mentalization-based treatment (MBT) framework, it is described along multiple dimensions, integrating cognitive, affective, relational, and regulatory processes. Large language models (LLMs) can generate linguistically reflective discourse, raising the question of whether the formal linguistic structure of mentalization can be reproduced without experiential and affective processes. Objective: This study aimed to assess whether an LLM can generate mentalization profiles structurally coherent with MBT dimensions and recognized as formally mentalizing by expert clinicians. Specifically, it evaluated the structural and argumentative coherence of model-generated profiles and the level of agreement among MBT-trained psychiatrists. It also examined the distinction between psychological mentalization and algorithmic reflectivity, conceptualized as a formally coherent but nonexperiential linguistic phenomenon. Methods: A comparative, descriptive methodological design was adopted. A total of 50 human-model dialogues were generated under standardized conditions. At the end of each interaction, the model produced a narrative mentalization profile structured along MBT dimensions. Five MBT-trained psychiatrists independently evaluated each profile using anonymized materials. Ratings covered 4 canonical MBT axes and 2 additional higher-order dimensions—regulatory functions and synthetic profile. For each dimension, they assigned separate Likert-scale ratings for evaluative and argumentative coherence. Descriptive statistics were computed at dialogue level. Interrater reliability was assessed using intraclass correlation coefficients (ICCs), primarily ICC(3,k), with CIs. The association between evaluative and argumentative coherence was examined using Pearson correlation coefficients with 95% CIs, with Spearman rank correlation as a robustness check. Results: Across dimensions, mean scores ranged from 3.63 (SD 0.63) to 3.98 (SD 0.31), indicating moderate-to-high structural coherence. Interrater reliability was moderate to good, with ICC(3,k) values ranging from 0.60 to 0.84 in the evaluative domain. The highest scores were observed for implicit-explicit and synthetic profile, whereas internal-external showed the lowest mean. Score distributions showed limited variability and concentration in the upper range of the scale. Evaluative and argumentative coherence were strongly associated across all MBT dimensions, with Pearson ranging from 0.826 to 0.967 (all &lt;.001) and Spearman ρ ranging from 0.866 to 0.991. Informal qualitative comments frequently described the outputs as linguistically organized and clinically interpretable, while also noting limited affective variability and contextual integration. Conclusions: The findings suggest that LLMs can reproduce the formal linguistic structure of reflective discourse, generating outputs perceived by clinicians as coherent and clinically interpretable across multiple dimensions of mentalization. However, within the present evaluative framework, this performance appears more consistent with structural language regularities than with evidence of experiential, affective, or intentional processes. These results support using LLMs as research and training tools for studying the linguistic aspects of mentalization, while highlighting the continuing importance of human clinical judgment in contexts requiring emotional, relational, and contextual integration. <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/e67bf7e4cea151dba5e47a406f7dbfd1" />

### [Diagnosis and Treatment of Cardiovascular Conditions in Medicare Advantage In-Home Health Risk Assessments.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-26-01366)

- Source: `annals_internal_abstracts` · published `2026-10-20` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42896184`

<h4>Background</h4>Medicare Advantage insurers conduct in-home health risk assessments (HRAs) to identify high-risk patients and facilitate appropriate care. Critics argue this practice increases risk-adjusted payments without improving quality. Comprehensive data on use of in-home HRAs and downstream treatment are lacking.<h4>Objective</h4>To examine trends in cardiovascular conditions diagnosed during in-home HRAs, characteristics associated with HRA-based diagnoses, and treatment rates after diagnoses.<h4>Design</h4>Cohort study.<h4>Setting</h4>Population-based.<h4>Participants</h4>Medicare Advantage beneficiaries.<h4>Measurements</h4>Using 100% Medicare claims, trends in the proportion of cardiovascular conditions diagnosed during in-home HRAs (vs. facility-based visits) were examined. Multivariable logistic regression models were fit to estimate the association between beneficiary- and plan-level characteristics and HRA-based diagnoses and compare treatment rates across settings.<h4>Results</h4>Among 21 496 506 Medicare Advantage beneficiaries diagnosed with a cardiovascular risk factor or disease, the proportion diagnosed during in-home HRAs increased between 2018 and 2022 for hypertension (45.9% to 47.7%), hyperlipidemia (57.8% to 66.7%), diabetes (47.8% to 51.6%), atrial fibrillation (30.0% to 36.0%), ischemic heart disease (37.2% to 41.6%), peripheral vascular disease (64.5% to 73.9%), and heart failure (34.7% to 42.6%). Racial and ethnic minority beneficiaries and those enrolled in lower-rated plans were more likely to receive HRA-based diagnoses. Treatment rates were substantially lower after HRA-based diagnoses across all conditions.<h4>Limitations</h4>Differences in the severity of conditions between groups could not be assessed. Prescription fills were used as a proxy for management. Observed differences in the diagnosis and treatment of conditions across encounter settings may be susceptible to unmeasured confounding.<h4>Conclusion</h4>Among Medicare Advantage beneficiaries, an increasingly large share of cardiovascular conditions are being diagnosed through in-home HRAs, but treatment rates after these diagnoses are consistently lower than after facility-based encounters.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>National Heart, Lung, and Blood Institute.

### [Effectiveness of a structured template to improve completeness of reporting randomised trial protocols: randomised controlled trial.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-100917)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-10-20` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42901609`

<h4>Objective</h4>To evaluate whether providing authors with a manuscript template structured according to the Standard Protocol Items: Recommendations for Interventional Trials (SPIRIT) 2025 reporting guideline for use during revision improves the completeness of reporting in randomised trial protocols.<h4>Design</h4>Randomised controlled trial.<h4>Setting</h4>BMJ Open.<h4>Population</h4>70 of 74 manuscripts screened between 29 April and 27 May 2025 that reported randomised controlled trial protocols with a decision of minor or major revision after the first round of peer review were randomly allocated (1:1) using an R generated sequence. Authors were not informed of the study.<h4>Interventions</h4>Handling editors asked the intervention group authors to use a SPIRIT based manuscript template when preparing their first revision. The template included structured subheadings corresponding to SPIRIT 2025 items, along with guidance on reporting. Editorial decisions were not conditional on use of the template. Control group manuscripts underwent standard peer review.<h4>Main outcome measures</h4>The primary outcome was completeness of reporting score in the revised manuscript (adjusted for baseline), calculated as the mean of 11 SPIRIT items that are frequently inadequately reported (each scored 0, 0.5, or 1) and linearly rescaled to a 0-10 score. After submission of their first revision, authors in the intervention group completed an online survey. The survey included both fixed response and open ended questions designed to capture the authors' perceptions of ease of use, helpfulness, and any challenges encountered when using the template. Secondary outcomes included mean completeness of reporting scores for each item, median time to submission of the revised manuscript, and authors' experiences of using the template. Outcome assessors, but not editors or investigators, were masked.<h4>Results</h4>Of the 70 manuscripts randomised (35 in each group), all were analysed, with no loss to follow-up. In the intervention group, completeness of reporting in randomised trial protocols improved by 0.61 points (95% confidence interval 0.20 to 1.01) compared with the control group, with mean (standard deviation) scores increasing from 5.42 (1.07) to 6.70 (1.23) and 5.77 (1.19) to 6.32 (0.94), respectively. The largest improvements were observed for recruitment, data collection methods, and access to data. Median time to submission of the first revision was similar between the intervention and control groups (27.10 <i>v</i> 29.86 days, respectively). Data on harms were not systematically collected. Among 14 of 35 survey respondents (40%), many (8/13, 62%) reported the template as time consuming and some (5/13, 39%) as burdensome. Most respondents, however, found it easy to use (10/13, 77%), helpful for improving the manuscript (9/13, 69%), and reassuring for adherence to SPIRIT (13/13, 100%).<h4>Conclusions</h4>Providing authors of randomised controlled trials with a SPIRIT based template during manuscript revision modestly improved the completeness of reporting in the trial protocols without extending revision times.<h4>Trial registration</h4>Open Science Framework 10.17605/OSF.IO/C4YQZ.

### [Mobile AI-Assisted Oral Lesion Triage for Community Oral Cancer Screening: Prospective Field Evaluation Study](https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e101721)

- Source: `jmir_mhealth` · published `2026-10-20` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e101721`

Background: AI-based tools for oral cancer screening have shown promising performance in curated image datasets, but their performance in real-world mobile community screening workflows remains less clear. Objective: This study evaluated the prospective field performance of a parameter-locked, mobile AI-assisted oral lesion triage system embedded in a human-supervised community oral cancer screening workflow. Methods: We conducted a prospective community-based field evaluation in eastern Taiwan from June 17 to December 31, 2025. Trained nonspecialist personnel acquired standard white-light intraoral images using handheld mobile devices during routine screening. The AI system incorporated on-site image quality assessment and lesion-level risk stratification into green, yellow, and red triage categories. Technically inadequate images after repeated acquisition attempts were classified as ungradable and excluded from performance analysis. Three board-certified oral and maxillofacial specialists established an operational clinical reference standard through structured image review and consensus adjudication. The primary outcome was lesion-level identification of high-risk lesions requiring specialist referral, defined as red versus nonred triage. Diagnostic performance was estimated with Wilson 95% CIs; precision-recall performance and preconsensus specialist agreement were also assessed. Results: Among 602 screened participants, 4283 interpretable intraoral images were included in expert adjudication and lesion-level analysis. The AI system flagged 68 red lesions; 12 (17.6%) were confirmed as high risk, and 4 additional high-risk lesions were identified during expert review, yielding 16 high-risk events for the primary red-triage analysis. For high-risk lesion identification, sensitivity was 75% (12/16; 95% CI 50.5%-89.8%) and positive predictive value was 17.6% (12/68; 95% CI 10.4%-28.4%); specificity, negative predictive value, and accuracy were 98.6% (3928/3984), 99.9% (3928/3932), and 98.5% (3940/4000), respectively. Area under the receiver operating characteristic curve values were 0.957 for red, 0.931 for yellow, and 0.964 for green triage; precision-recall analysis showed area under the precision-recall curve values of 0.8235, 0.9052, and 0.9476, respectively. Preconsensus specialist agreement was high, with a Fleiss κ of 0.921 and a Gwet AC1 of 0.981. Conclusions: This mobile AI-assisted oral lesion triage system was feasibly integrated into a real-world community screening workflow operated by trained nonspecialist personnel. In this low-prevalence field setting, red triage showed moderate sensitivity and modest positive predictive value, supporting its use as human-supervised referral-prioritization support rather than autonomous diagnosis. Further multicenter studies with blinded assessment, participant-level outcomes, workflow-efficiency measures, and longitudinal follow-up are needed before clinical impact and scalability can be determined.

### [Overuse and False Restraint in Large Language Model Recommendations for Choosing Wisely Scenarios.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-26-01667)

- Source: `annals_internal_abstracts` · published `2026-10-20` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42896192`

<h4>Background</h4>The Choosing Wisely initiative made recommendations where screening, testing, or treatment may be overused, with caveats around appropriate use. Clinicians are increasingly using large language models (LLMs).<h4>Objective</h4>To evaluate whether commercial LLMs appropriately call for restraint or action from scenarios based on Choosing Wisely recommendations.<h4>Design</h4>Benchmark evaluation. Fifteen Choosing Wisely recommendations were used to develop 225 scenarios (per recommendation, 10 in which restraint was appropriate and 5 in which action was appropriate). Six commercial LLMs (3 runs each) generated 4050 responses to the 225 scenarios, using a prompt from the clinician perspective.<h4>Setting</h4>Production application programming interfaces of OpenAI, Anthropic, and Google from February to March 2026.<h4>Participants</h4>No human participants were involved.<h4>Measurements</h4>Co-primary outcomes were the overuse rate (endorsing action when restraint was appropriate) and the false restraint rate (withholding an appropriate action).<h4>Results</h4>The rate of LLM-recommended overuse was 5.4% (95% CI, 0.6% to 12.2%); LLMs did not recommend overuse in 8 of 15 recommendations. Two recommendations accounted for most LLM-recommended overuse: thyroid ultrasound for an incidental subcentimeter nodule (overuse recommended in 42.8% of LLM responses) and routine electrocardiography in low-risk adults (overuse recommended in 28.3% of LLM responses). The LLMs recommended overuse in 0.6% to 3.3% of responses for the remaining 5 recommendations. False restraint was rare (0.6% [CI, 0.1% to 1.2%]), was absent for 11 of 15 recommendations, and was highest for prostate-specific antigen screening (3.3%).<h4>Limitations</h4>Single prompts without follow-up; a benchmark rather than real-world use; responses classified by LLM judge with physician adjudication. Findings may not extend to consumer-facing use.<h4>Conclusion</h4>The LLMs rarely withheld recommendations for treatment when it was appropriate. Overuse was concentrated in 2 recommendations.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>None.

### [Projected Effect of 2026 Federal Changes to the Childhood Immunization Schedule on Chronic Hepatitis B Virus Infections in the United States.](https://doi.org/10.7326/annals-26-02135)

- Source: `annals_internal_abstracts` · published `2026-10-20` · freshness `stale`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `ineligible`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42896183`

<h4>Background</h4>The United States recently proposed changes to the recommendations for childhood hepatitis B virus (HBV) vaccination.<h4>Objective</h4>To project the effect of vaccine policy changes, if fully implemented, on chronic HBV infections.<h4>Design</h4>Markov cohort state transition model of perinatal and childhood HBV infection.<h4>Data sources</h4>Published data on maternal HBV prevalence (0.4%) and hepatitis B surface antigen testing (99.5% sensitivity), childhood vaccine completion (universal birth-dose strategy: 91.1%; deferred strategy: 87.1%; shared decision-making strategy: 50.0%), and age-stratified risk for chronic HBV infection.<h4>Target population</h4>One-year birth cohort of U.S.-born infants (<i>n</i> = 3.6 million).<h4>Time horizon</h4>20 years.<h4>Intervention</h4>Universal birth-dose vaccination, deferred (2-month) vaccination, and vaccination through shared decision making.<h4>Outcome measures</h4>Numbers with chronic HBV infection and susceptibility to HBV, with 95% uncertainty intervals (UIs) derived from probabilistic sensitivity analysis.<h4>Results of base-case analysis</h4>Compared with the universal birth-dose strategy (255 childhood infections [95% UI, 130 to 530]), the deferred and shared decision-making strategies would increase chronic HBV infections in the 2026 birth cohort by 19% to 303 (95% UI, 155 to 610) and by 83% to 467 (95% UI, 209 to 1032), respectively. These strategies would also increase the number of HBV-susceptible people at year 20 (474 000 and 1 806 000) compared with the universal birth-dose strategy (232 000).<h4>Results of sensitivity analysis</h4>In a scenario with lower vaccine coverage (84.5%) and higher HBV prevalence (maternal prevalence of 0.72% and higher risk for horizontal infection), chronic HBV infections would increase to 1700 and 2192, respectively, with the deferred and shared decision-making strategies.<h4>Limitation</h4>Uncertainty in horizontal HBV infection risk.<h4>Conclusion</h4>Proposed policy changes for vaccination through shared decision making are projected to result in an 83% increase in childhood HBV infections, and between 50% and 70% of children entering adulthood annually would be susceptible to HBV infection. Policymakers should reimplement recommendations for universal birth-dose vaccination.<h4>Primary funding source</h4>National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases.

### [Endometrial ablation plus levonorgestrel-releasing intrauterine system versus endometrial ablation alone in women with heavy menstrual bleeding in The Netherlands (MIRA2): a multicentre, open-label, randomised, controlled, superiority trial.](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0140-6736(26)01536-9)

- Source: `lancet_abstracts` · published `2026-10-21` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `69` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42906565`

<h4>Background</h4>Endometrial ablation is an established and effective treatment for women with heavy menstrual bleeding, but many patients require further treatment, with approximately 10% ultimately undergoing a hysterectomy within 2 years. The insertion of a levonorgestrel-releasing intrauterine system (LNG-IUS) immediately after endometrial ablation might inactivate residual untreated endometrium or prevent its regeneration, thereby preventing recurrent symptoms necessitating a hysterectomy. The aim of the study was to assess whether LNG-IUS insertion immediately after endometrial ablation in women with heavy menstrual bleeding reduced the need for subsequent hysterectomy within 2 years compared with endometrial ablation alone.<h4>Methods</h4>This multicentre, open-label, randomised, controlled, superiority trial was conducted in 34 hospitals in the Netherlands. Women younger than 60 years with heavy menstrual bleeding who were eligible for endometrial ablation were included and randomly assigned (1:1) to combined treatment comprising endometrial ablation plus LNG-IUS or to standard care comprising endometrial ablation alone (stratified by dysmenorrhoea and age). Masking was not applied. Endometrial ablation was done using electrical radiofrequency ablation or by transcervical resection of the endometrium. In participants allocated to combined treatment, intrauterine insertion of an LNG-IUS containing a total of 52 mg of levonorgestrel (releasing 20 μg per day in the first year) was done immediately after the ablative procedure. The primary outcome was the incidence of hysterectomy within 2 years, analysed on a modified intention-to-treat basis in all participants with available data. Serious adverse events were systematically assessed at 6 weeks, 12 months, and 24 months after the intervention. This study was registered on the International Clinical Trials Registry Platform (NL-OMON54839) and is complete.<h4>Findings</h4>Between Nov 19, 2019, and April 5, 2023, 715 participants were randomly assigned to combined treatment (n=359) or endometrial ablation alone (n=356). The median age of all participants was 45 years (IQR 40-48). 42 (6%) participants had missing data for the primary outcome at 2 years of follow-up. A hysterectomy was done in 15 (4%) of 342 participants in the combined treatment group versus 34 (10%) of 331 participants in the endometrial ablation group, corresponding to a relative risk (RR) of 0·43 (95% CI 0·24 to 0·77; p=0·0050), with a risk difference of -0·06 (95% CI -0·10 to -0·02; p=0·0040). Serious adverse events were reported in 27 (8%) of 346 participants in the combined treatment group and 26 (8%) of 327 participants in the endometrial ablation alone group (RR 0·98, 95% CI 0·59-1·65; p=0·94), and there were no treatment-related deaths.<h4>Interpretation</h4>The combination of endometrial ablation and LNG-IUS significantly reduced the incidence of hysterectomy compared with endometrial ablation alone. The combined treatment appears to be a safe adjunctive strategy for heavy menstrual bleeding that does not increase the risk of complications.<h4>Funding</h4>The Netherlands Organization for Health Research and Development.

### [Obesity in Children Aged 2 to 12 Years: A Review.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.18806)

- Source: `jama_abstracts` · published `2026-10-21` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `57` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42905688`

<h4>Importance</h4>Obesity affects 15% of children aged 2 to 5 years and 22% of children aged 6 to 11 years in the US. Obesity in children is associated with dyslipidemia, hypertension, metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease, and obstructive sleep apnea. Compared with normal-weight peers, children with obesity have lower quality of life and are 5 times more likely to have obesity in adulthood.<h4>Observations</h4>Body mass index (BMI; calculated as weight in kilograms divided by height in meters squared) is used to identify children at risk for excess adiposity, and guidelines recommend that clinicians screen for obesity using BMI starting at age 2 years. Among children, obesity is defined using standardized age- and sex-specific growth charts, with obesity defined as BMI in the 95th or higher percentile and severe obesity as BMI of 120% or greater of the 95th percentile. Obesity treatment in children includes intensive health behavior and lifestyle treatment, consisting of at least 26 contact hours of face-to-face group or family-based activities that incorporate nutrition, physical activity, and behavior change education delivered to the child and caregivers over a 3- to 12-month period. Intensive health behavior and lifestyle treatment reduces BMI by 0.7 compared with children who do not participate in intensive health behavior and lifestyle treatment. The only obesity pharmacotherapy currently approved by the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) for children younger than 12 years is setmelanotide, a melanocortin-4 receptor agonist, which is indicated for 3 forms of rare monogenic obesity (LEPR, POMC, and PCSK1 genetic variants), Bardet-Biedl syndrome, and acquired hypothalamic obesity. Liraglutide is currently only FDA approved for youth aged 12 years or older; however, a randomized clinical trial among children aged 6 to younger than 12 years demonstrated that, compared with placebo, liraglutide reduced BMI by 7.4%. Metabolic and bariatric surgery is rarely used in children younger than age 12 years and evidence supporting its use in children is limited. Early identification and treatment of obesity during childhood may reduce risk of type 2 diabetes, dyslipidemia, hypertension, and mortality in adulthood.<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>Obesity in children is associated with chronic disease, such as hypertension, and with poor quality of life. Evidence-based treatment for obesity in children includes intensive health behavior and lifestyle treatment, and pharmacotherapy may provide additional benefit, although short- and long-term safety and efficacy data are limited. Early identification and treatment of obesity during childhood may reduce risk of type 2 diabetes, dyslipidemia, hypertension, and mortality in adulthood.

### [Configurations of Quality Dimensions Associated With Researcher and Patient Ratings of HIV/AIDS Mobile Health Apps in China: Fuzzy-Set Qualitative Comparative Analysis](https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e88896)

- Source: `jmir_mhealth` · published `2026-10-22` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `49` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://mhealth.jmir.org/2026/1/e88896`

<strong>Background:</strong> Mobile health (mHealth) apps have become important tools for HIV/AIDS management. Although the Mobile App Rating Scale and its user version are widely used, how different stakeholder groups combine quality dimensions into overall judgments remains unclear. Discrepancies between researcher and patient evaluations may affect app adoption and clinical effectiveness. <strong>Objective:</strong> This exploratory study aimed to compare configurational pathways associated with high subjective quality ratings of HIV/AIDS apps between researchers and patients using fuzzy-set qualitative comparative analysis. <strong>Methods:</strong> Twenty-one Chinese HIV/AIDS mobile apps from early 2023 were evaluated by 3 researchers using the original Mobile App Rating Scale and 2 HIV-positive patients using the user version of the Mobile App Rating Scale. Six conditions were examined: engagement, functionality, aesthetics, information quality, privacy protection, and developer type. Necessary and sufficient configurations were identified for both groups. Wilcoxon signed-rank tests compared raw ratings between groups. Sensitivity analyses covered 4 calibration anchor sets, 4 raw-consistency cutoffs, 4 proportional reduction in inconsistency cutoffs, and multivalue operationalization of privacy protection. <strong>Results:</strong> Researcher and patient ratings differed significantly for functionality (median difference 0.50, IQR 0.00 to 0.63; <i>P</i>=.003) and engagement (−0.20, IQR −0.40 to 0.10; <i>P</i>=.04), but not for aesthetics (<i>P</i>=.16), information (<i>P</i>=.07), or subjective quality (<i>P</i>=.06). Five sufficient configurations were identified for researchers (solution consistency=0.981, coverage=0.649) and 4 for patients (solution consistency=0.998, coverage=0.796). Configurations including privacy protection appeared only among researchers; its absence appeared in 3 patient configurations, reflecting the rarity of protection in the sample (5/21 apps) rather than lower patient ratings for protected apps. For patients, functionality (0.907) and information quality (0.915) exceeded the necessity threshold but were strongly correlated (<i>r</i>=0.816), so functionality was retained on substantive grounds rather than as an independent necessary condition. Illustrative cases were Wu Ai Fang Hua and Ai Yi Jian for the researcher-oriented pathway and Chabei for the patient-oriented pathway. Sensitivity analyses left the configurations unchanged across all anchor sets and raw-consistency cutoffs; 1 researcher configuration did not survive the stricter proportional reduction in inconsistency cutoffs, and the patient necessity result was anchor-dependent. <strong>Conclusions:</strong> The 2 evaluator panels’ assessments of HIV/AIDS mHealth apps appeared to follow different configurational logic in this sample, with several equifinal pathways to high subjective quality in each group. Because the patient panel comprised 2 evaluators, and 21 apps were analyzed with 6 conditions, the findings should be read as hypothesis-generating rather than representative of people living with HIV. The findings nonetheless suggest that both expert and end-user perspectives are needed during mHealth development, and that, because privacy protection was rare and not required for a favorable patient judgment, its provision in a population highly vulnerable to disclosure-related harm needs to be ensured by developers and regulators rather than left to user demand.

### [Large Language Model–Assisted Semiautomated Data Harmonization Pipeline for the Multiple Chronic Disease Disparities Research Consortium: Framework Development and Comparative Validation Study](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e93996/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-10-22` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `63` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e93996/`

Background: The increasing availability of machine-readable research data has created a growing need for efficient large-scale data harmonization (DH). Although large language models (LLMs) show promise for reducing the time and labor required for DH, their effective integration into harmonization workflows remains an important methodological challenge. Objective: This study aims to develop and evaluate a semiautomated, human-in-the-loop (HITL) LLM-assisted DH workflow as a proof of concept across 8 projects within a research consortium focused on disparities in multiple chronic conditions. Methods: We developed an LLM-assisted DH workflow that combined preprocessing, semantic mapping, variable response mapping, synthetic data generation, data transformation, and iterative researcher review. Using Generative Pretrained Transformer (GPT-4o) hosted in a secure academic environment, project-specific survey items were mapped to 114 Consortium Common Data Element (CDE) semantic groups. Semantic mapping accuracy was evaluated based on the Consortium’s consensus harmonization results. Complete and incomplete semantic mappings were distinguished according to construct overlap, and incomplete mappings requiring context-dependent human judgment were excluded for mapped item pairs and CDE semantic groups with and without HITL review. Results: Following preprocessing, 884 survey items were included in the DH workflow, of which 812 were mapped to a mean of 79 CDE semantic groups per project. Mean semantic mapping accuracy for item pairs was 95.9% (SD 5.7%) with HITL review and 86.7% (SD 8.6%) without HITL review. At the CDE semantic-group level, the corresponding accuracies were 98.9% (SD 1.7%) and 94.9% (SD 4.0%), respectively. Across projects, omission of valid semantic mappings occurred more frequently than incorrect semantic mappings, particularly for heterogeneous response structures and subjective constructs measured using different instruments. Pipeline components—including concept-based preprocessing, iterative prompt refinement, and targeted HITL review—improved mapping completeness and supported variable harmonization across heterogeneous datasets. Conclusions: This proof-of-concept study demonstrates that the effectiveness of LLM-assisted DH depends more on the design of a structured HITL workflow rather than on the LLM alone. Combining preprocessing, iterative verification, and targeted human oversight improved the efficiency and reliability of DH while preserving researcher judgment for complex mapping decisions. <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/73dab303e7a1c55be905e262200f6a57" />

### [Optimizing Small Local Language Models for Culturally Competent Mental Health Counseling: Comparative Evaluation With GPT-4o by Psychiatrists](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e92470/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-10-22` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `63` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e92470/`

&lt;strong&gt;Background:&lt;/strong&gt; Large language models (LLMs) show promise for mental health counseling applications. However, their deployment faces significant challenges, including data privacy concerns requiring on-premise solutions, high operational costs, and Western-centric biases that limit cultural appropriateness for non–English-speaking populations. &lt;strong&gt;Objective:&lt;/strong&gt; This study aimed to develop and evaluate culturally competent small language models (SLMs) with approximately 7 to 8 billion parameters for Korean mental health counseling and to validate a 2-stage hybrid evaluation framework combining LLM-as-a-Judge screening with expert clinical validation. &lt;strong&gt;Methods:&lt;/strong&gt; We constructed a dataset of 1558 diary-based counseling interactions from a Korean mental health mobile app (Maeumpyeonuijeom). Three Korean-optimized base models (SKT A.X 4.0 Light {7B}, LG EXAONE 3.5 {7.8B}, and Meta Llama 3.1 {8B}) were fine-tuned using an aggressive fine-tuning strategy with high-rank low-rank adaptation (&lt;i&gt;r&lt;/i&gt;=256) over 20 epochs. Inference parameters were optimized using GPT-5 (gpt-5-2025-08-07) as an LLM-as-a-Judge. The optimized SLMs and GPT-4o (gpt-4o-2024-08-06) were evaluated by 10 board-certified psychiatrists (mean clinical experience of approximately 10 years) on 50 counseling scenarios across 4 dimensions (individualization, supportiveness, cultural appropriateness, and safety) using 5-point Likert scales. Wilcoxon signed-rank tests with Holm correction were used for statistical comparison, and intraclass correlation coefficients (ICCs) assessed interrater reliability. &lt;strong&gt;Results:&lt;/strong&gt; No statistically significant difference from GPT-4o was detected for SKT A.X 4.0 Light in supportiveness (mean 3.39, SD 0.74 vs mean 3.45, SD 0.90; &lt;i&gt;P&lt;/i&gt;=.26) and cultural appropriateness (mean 3.30, SD 0.77 vs mean 3.29, SD 0.79; &lt;i&gt;P&lt;/i&gt;=.58). SKT A.X 4.0 Light ranked first in cultural appropriateness among all models. Interrater reliability analysis revealed higher agreement among psychiatrists for local SLMs (ICC=0.575 for SKT A.X 4.0 Light in cultural appropriateness) compared with GPT-4o (ICC=0.211). GPT-4o maintained significantly higher safety scores than all SLMs (&lt;i&gt;P&lt;/i&gt;&amp;lt;.001). The LLM-as-a-Judge demonstrated moderate-to-good correlation with human evaluation but exhibited a ceiling effect in safety scoring. &lt;strong&gt;Conclusions:&lt;/strong&gt; A 7 to 8-billion-parameter SLM fine-tuned on culturally specific data can achieve performance comparable to GPT-4o in supportiveness and cultural appropriateness for mental health counseling. These findings support the feasibility of locally deployable, privacy-preserving mental health AI systems based on culturally adapted SLMs. However, the safety gap necessitates human expert oversight, and success depends on the availability of high-quality language-specific base models. <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/20afe95ce61e268386ede2b59a9ea535" />

### [Pregnancy Outcomes After Living Kidney Donation.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.21737)

- Source: `jama_abstracts` · published `2026-10-22` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `65` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42914154`

<h4>Importance</h4>People who become pregnant after living kidney donation may be at increased risk of pregnancy-related adverse outcomes, although the evidence is unclear.<h4>Objective</h4>To determine whether pregnancies after living kidney donation are associated with a higher risk of adverse pregnancy outcomes than matched pregnancies among nondonors.<h4>Design, setting, and participants</h4>This matched cohort study used provincial administrative health care databases from Ontario, British Columbia, and Alberta, Canada, and included living female kidney donors aged 18 to 42 years who donated a kidney between July 1992 and August 2021 and subsequently had singleton pregnancies of at least 20 weeks' gestation. These pregnancies were matched 1:4 with singleton pregnancies of at least 20 weeks' gestation among female nondonors. Pregnancies in donors and nondonors were matched on delivery date, age at delivery, years between cohort entry and delivery, number of prior pregnancies, and income. Final follow-up occurred until March 2023. Outcome analysis occurred from August 2024 to September 2026.<h4>Main outcomes and measures</h4>The primary outcome was a composite of hospitalization with a diagnosis of gestational hypertension, preeclampsia, or eclampsia. Secondary outcomes included 12 maternal, fetal and neonatal adverse outcomes, including the individual components of the primary outcome, the composite of low birth weight or preterm birth, and small for gestational age at birth.<h4>Results</h4>The sample included 385 pregnancies in 252 living kidney donors (mean [SD] age, 27.8 [5.0] years) matched to 1540 pregnancies in 1535 nondonors (mean [SD] age, 27.0 [4.8] years). The primary composite outcome of gestational hypertension, preeclampsia, or eclampsia occurred in 46 donor pregnancies (11.9%) and 128 nondonor pregnancies (8.3%; risk ratio, 1.51; 95% CI, 1.10-2.08). Among the 12 secondary outcomes, the 95% CI for the risk ratio excluded 1.00 for the outcome of small for gestational age, which occurred in 53 donor pregnancies (13.8%) and 121 nondonor pregnancies (7.9%) (risk ratio, 1.86; 95% CI, 1.37-2.52).<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>In this study, pregnancy after living kidney donation was associated with a higher risk of the composite outcome of hospitalization with a diagnosis of gestational hypertension, preeclampsia, or eclampsia, when compared with matched pregnancies in nondonors. Most postdonation pregnancies were uncomplicated. These findings support counseling donor candidates who may become pregnant about this risk and informing the prenatal care of donors who become pregnant.

### [Deriving a Deployment Threshold for Benefit and Harm in Clinical AI: Multi-Institutional Decision-Analytic Modeling Study](https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e109863/)

- Source: `jmir_ai` · published `2026-10-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `66` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://ai.jmir.org/2026/1/e109863/`

&lt;strong&gt;Background:&lt;/strong&gt; Clinical AI operates in a health care environment heterogeneous in biology, clinical practice, and documentation, so its benefit-harm balance is deployment-specific: an intervention can help in one setting and harm in another when the determinant of response is not routinely observed. &lt;strong&gt;Objective:&lt;/strong&gt; This study had 2 aims: to derive the deployment threshold at which an AI intervention whose effect changes sign becomes beneficial, using zero-phase component analysis whitening (a retraining-free correction to clinical retrieval embeddings) as a worked case, and to determine whether the evidence needed to apply that threshold exists in the published literature or must be measured locally. &lt;strong&gt;Methods:&lt;/strong&gt; We specified a 3-arm decision-analytic model—no action, universal deployment, and screen-and-treat—for interventions with sign-changing effects and instantiated it on a retraining-free correction to clinical retrieval embeddings. Empirical effect groups were defined by measured response rather than nominal mechanistic tier. Screening accuracy was derived from between-condition variability and tested by local resampling. Transportability was evaluated on emergency-department notes and discharge summaries from 2 US academic centers. A structural sensitivity analysis allowed the downstream value of benefit and harm to differ. &lt;strong&gt;Results:&lt;/strong&gt; Measured response defined 6 benefited and 7 harmed configurations at the base-case regularization. The universal-deployment threshold was &lt;i&gt;p&lt;/i&gt;*=0.50; the exact and zero-cost forms differ by 2.8×10&lt;sup&gt;–5&lt;/sup&gt;, so strategy cost had a negligible effect on it, and across 6 regularization settings, the threshold ranged from 0.32 to 0.70. Published descriptions did not identify the response-defined state: none of the 55 published systems had been evaluated under the intervention, so response prevalence remained unidentified without classification assumptions. Local screening reduced the minimum required prior probability from 0.210 with 1 scored condition to 0.071 with 6. The nominal mechanistic tier agreed with response in all 52 institutional section-level cells, but 1 benchmark-harmed configuration benefited in all 4 institutional conditions. In the joint fit-and-evaluation resampling protocol, specificity rose from 0.328 at 10 documents to 0.974 at 75 documents. &lt;strong&gt;Conclusions:&lt;/strong&gt; The deployment threshold is analytically identifiable, but the state needed to apply; it cannot be recovered reliably from model identity or the published literature. Deployment validity is therefore local in the setting examined: local measurement is needed when the response-defining state cannot otherwise be established reliably before deployment, and an unsuitable measured state requires local action rather than plug-and-play transfer. The data do not identify any single adaptation method as universally necessary. &lt;strong&gt;Trial Registration:&lt;/strong&gt; <img src="https://jmir-production.s3.us-east-2.amazonaws.com/thumbs/78fba0a4f5a38d7e2cda6240253399df" />

### [Hydrogel for sustained and localized delivery of a regulatory T cell-inducing agent promotes bone regeneration](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01817-1)

- Source: `nature_biomedical_engineering` · published `2026-10-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `61` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `url:https://www.nature.com/articles/s41551-026-01817-1`

Local activation of regulatory T (Treg) cells represents a promising strategy for wound repair and tissue regeneration. However, current Treg-based therapies rely on adoptive cell transfer, which requires ex vivo expansion and reinfusion, posing challenges to donor variability and tissue specificity. Here we engineer a hydrogel-based system for in situ Treg activation at injury sites through the controlled release of AS2863619 (AS), a cyclin-dependent kinase inhibitor. AS is incorporated into collagen hydrogels at concentrations exceeding its solubility limit, reducing burst release and extending the release half-life. This continuous exposure to Treg-inducing agents stabilizes Foxp3 expression in CD4+ T cells, thereby enabling Treg induction and stabilization. The incorporation of laponite nanoparticles further slows AS release and prolongs drug retention. In a calvarial bone-defect model, AS-loaded hydrogels induce dose-dependent Treg cell activation, reduce pro-inflammatory macrophage responses and enhance bone regeneration. Notably, AS–laponite hydrogels achieve complete defect bridging in some cases. By providing spatiotemporal control over Treg populations without the need for cell isolation and expansion, this approach offers a scalable alternative for immunomodulation and tissue repair. A laponite nanoparticles-based hydrogel loaded with a cyclin-dependent kinase inhibitor induces localized regulatory T (Treg) cell activation directly in vivo and enhances bone regeneration in a calvarial bone-defect mouse model.

### [Mechanical Thrombectomy 24 to 72 Hours after Large-Vessel Ischemic Stroke.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2609362)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42929656`

<h4>Background</h4>Endovascular thrombectomy is an established treatment for acute ischemic stroke. Whether the benefit extends to patients with salvageable brain tissue in the infarct who present more than 24 hours after symptom onset or the time when they were last known to be well is uncertain.<h4>Methods</h4>We conducted an open-label, randomized trial with blinded outcome assessment at comprehensive stroke centers in China. Patients with acute ischemic stroke from large-vessel occlusion of the anterior circulation and imaging-identified salvageable brain tissue at 24 to 72 hours were assigned to receive endovascular therapy plus standard medical care (intervention group) or standard medical care alone (control group). The primary outcome was functional status, defined by the score on the modified Rankin scale (range, 0 to 6, with higher scores indicating greater disability and a score of 6 indicating death) at 90 days, with results assessed as the distribution of scores in each group. Secondary outcomes included disability, neurologic impairment, and safety.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 336 patients were included in the intention-to-treat population, with 168 assigned to each group. The median time from symptom onset or from the point when the patient was last known to be well to randomization was 38.5 hours. Endovascular therapy resulted in a more favorable distribution of modified Rankin scale scores at 90 days than standard medical care alone (common odds ratio for a better functional outcome, 1.74; 95% confidence interval, 1.19 to 2.55). Symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage occurred in 4.8% of the patients in the intervention group and in no patients in the control group, and the 90-day case fatality rate was 6.0% and 1.8%, respectively.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Among patients who presented 24 to 72 hours after an acute ischemic stroke from large-vessel occlusion of the anterior circulation and had imaging-identified salvageable tissue, endovascular therapy resulted in better functional outcomes than standard care but led to a higher risk of intracranial hemorrhage and death. (Funded by the National Health Commission Capacity Building and Continuing Education Center and others; LATE-MT ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT05326932.).

### [Trial of Endovascular Therapy for Mild Stroke with Large-Vessel Occlusion.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2609149)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-23` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42929671`

<h4>Background</h4>Whether endovascular therapy benefits patients with mild acute ischemic stroke from large-vessel occlusion involving the anterior circulation is uncertain.<h4>Methods</h4>We conducted a multicenter, open-label, randomized trial in China involving patients with acute ischemic stroke of mild clinical severity (defined by a score of <6 on the National Institutes of Health Stroke Scale) that had resulted from large-vessel occlusion involving the anterior circulation. Patients also had neuroimaging features of a small infarct core and a large perfusion-mismatch volume, representing substantial at-risk tissue, within 24 hours after symptom onset or after they were last known to be well. The patients were randomly assigned to undergo endovascular therapy and receive medical management or to receive medical management alone. The primary outcome was an excellent functional outcome (defined by a modified Rankin scale score of 0 [no symptoms] or 1 [no clinically significant disability]) at 90 days.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 304 patients underwent randomization, of whom 300 were included in the modified intention-to-treat population: 144 in the endovascular-therapy group and 156 in the medical-management group. At 90 days, a modified Rankin scale score of 0 or 1 was reported in 69.4% of the patients in the endovascular-therapy group and in 50.0% of those in the medical-management group (odds ratio, 2.32; 95% confidence interval, 1.41 to 3.81; P<0.001). Symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage within 48 hours occurred in 3 patients in each group. Serious procedure-related complications occurred in 19 patients (13.2%) in the endovascular-therapy group. Among all the participants, one death occurred (in the endovascular-therapy group).<h4>Conclusions</h4>In this trial from China involving patients with mild acute ischemic stroke caused by large-vessel occlusion and substantial at-risk tissue on perfusion imaging, endovascular therapy performed within 24 hours after symptom onset plus medical management resulted in better 90-day functional outcomes than medical management alone, without an increase in the risk of symptomatic intracranial hemorrhage but with a high incidence of serious procedural complications. (Funded by Fujian Medical University Union Hospital and others; MILD-MT ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT06179017.).

### [Anbenitamab plus HB1801 for HER2-Positive Advanced Breast Cancer.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2610345)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`

<h4>Background</h4>The combination of docetaxel, trastuzumab, and pertuzumab (THP) is an established first-line treatment for human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-positive advanced breast cancer. The efficacy and safety of anbenitamab, a HER2-targeted bispecific antibody, plus albumin-bound docetaxel (HB1801) in this context are unknown.<h4>Methods</h4>In this phase 3, multicenter, open-label, randomized trial conducted in China, we randomly assigned patients with HER2-positive advanced breast cancer in a 1:1 ratio to receive either anbenitamab plus HB1801 or THP. The primary end point was progression-free survival, as assessed by blinded independent central review. Objective response was a secondary end point.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 885 patients were enrolled (445 were assigned to receive anbenitamab plus HB1801 and 440 to receive THP). At this prespecified interim analysis (with 401 events [disease progression or death] and a median follow-up of 21.0 months), the estimated 24-month progression-free survival was significantly higher with anbenitamab plus HB1801 than with THP (58.5% vs. 34.4%; hazard ratio for progression or death, 0.46; 95% confidence interval, 0.37 to 0.56; P<0.001). The percentage of patients with an objective response was 87.6% in the anbenitamab group and 73.4% in the THP group. Adverse events of grade 3 or higher occurred in 204 of 444 patients (45.9%) in the anbenitamab group and in 151 of 439 patients (34.4%) in the THP group. Neutropenia was the most common adverse event of grade 3 or higher in both groups (13.1% in the anbenitamab group and 8.4% in the THP group). Adverse events leading to death occurred in 2 patients in each group.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Anbenitamab plus HB1801 led to significantly higher progression-free survival than THP among patients with HER2-positive advanced breast cancer. (Funded by Shanghai JMT-Bio Technology and others; HEALER ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT05838066.).

### [Durvalumab, Tremelimumab, and Enfortumab Vedotin in Muscle-Invasive Bladder Cancer.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2608323)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42929692`

<h4>Background</h4>The standard of care for patients with muscle-invasive bladder cancer continues to evolve. Whether perioperative durvalumab, with or without tremelimumab, plus neoadjuvant enfortumab vedotin is effective in such patients is unknown.<h4>Methods</h4>In this phase 3 open-label trial, we randomly assigned, in a 1:1:1 ratio, patients with muscle-invasive bladder cancer who were ineligible for or declined cisplatin-based chemotherapy to receive neoadjuvant durvalumab-enfortumab vedotin every 3 weeks for three cycles, followed by radical cystectomy and then adjuvant durvalumab every 4 weeks for nine cycles; neoadjuvant durvalumab-tremelimumab-enfortumab vedotin (tremelimumab for two cycles only), radical cystectomy, and adjuvant durvalumab-tremelimumab (tremelimumab for one cycle only); or radical cystectomy alone (control). The dual primary end points were event-free survival in each of the two durvalumab groups as compared with that in the control group. The two durvalumab groups were not compared with each other. The key secondary end point was overall survival.<h4>Results</h4>In total, 233 patients were assigned to the durvalumab-enfortumab vedotin group, 233 to the durvalumab-tremelimumab-enfortumab vedotin group, and 229 to the control group. Estimated event-free survival at 24 months was 63.5% (95% confidence interval [CI], 56.5 to 69.7) with durvalumab-enfortumab vedotin and 45.1% (95% CI, 38.2 to 51.8) with control (hazard ratio for an event or death, 0.51; 95% CI, 0.38 to 0.67; P<0.001); estimated overall survival at 24 months was 78.4% and 69.4%, respectively (hazard ratio for death, 0.58; 95% CI, 0.40 to 0.83; P = 0.003). Estimated event-free survival at 24 months was 60.1% (95% CI, 53.2 to 66.2) with durvalumab-tremelimumab-enfortumab vedotin (hazard ratio for an event or death vs. control, 0.61; 95% CI, 0.47 to 0.80; P<0.001). Grade 3 or higher adverse events occurred in 71.1% of patients in the durvalumab-enfortumab vedotin group (treatment-related, 30.7%), in 79.5% of those in the durvalumab-tremelimumab-enfortumab vedotin group (treatment-related, 44.5%), and in 50.2% of those in the control group.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Perioperative durvalumab with or without tremelimumab plus three cycles of neoadjuvant enfortumab vedotin resulted in significantly better event-free survival outcomes than surgery alone. Perioperative durvalumab plus enfortumab vedotin led to significantly better overall survival outcomes than surgery alone. Grade 3 or higher adverse events of any cause were noted in more than half the patients in the durvalumab groups. (Funded by AstraZeneca; VOLGA ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT04960709; EudraCT number, 2020-005452-38.).

### [Sodium-Glucose Cotransporter 2 Inhibitor Use and Levels of Hemoglobin, Markers of Iron Metabolism, and Erythropoiesis: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Randomized Clinical Trials.](https://doi.org/10.1001/jama.2026.19921)

- Source: `jama_abstracts` · published `2026-10-24` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `63` · status: `scored`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42925557`

<h4>Importance</h4>In randomized clinical trials, sodium-glucose cotransporter 2 (SGLT2) inhibitor use was associated with increased hemoglobin and hematocrit compared with placebo. However, the magnitude and importance of this association remain unclear.<h4>Objective</h4>To evaluate the associations between SGLT2 inhibitor use and levels of hemoglobin, hematocrit, and markers of iron metabolism and erythropoiesis compared with placebo or active comparator.<h4>Data sources</h4>PubMed, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials (CENTRAL) were searched from inception to July 22, 2026.<h4>Study selection</h4>Randomized, double-masked clinical trials comparing SGLT2 inhibitor use with placebo or active comparators in patients with 1 or more of the following conditions: cardiovascular disease, diabetes, heart failure, and chronic kidney disease.<h4>Data extraction and synthesis</h4>Three reviewers independently extracted data and results were pooled using random-effects meta-analysis.<h4>Main outcomes and measures</h4>Primary outcomes were changes in hemoglobin and hematocrit. Secondary outcomes were changes in red blood cell concentration, erythropoietin (EPO), and iron measures (serum iron, ferritin, transferrin saturation, and hepcidin).<h4>Results</h4>A total of 85 randomized clinical trials were analyzed (median [IQR] duration, 24 [12-32] weeks), including 70 543 participants (mean [SD] age, 62.4 [9.4] years; 25 334 female [35.9%]). Mean (SD) baseline hemoglobin was 13.6 (1.5) g/dL; baseline hematocrit was 41.5% (4.9). Seventy-eight clinical trials (67 208 participants) compared SGLT2 inhibitors with placebo, and 7 clinical trials (3335 participants) used active comparators, most commonly sitagliptin (2 trials [1158 participants]). From baseline to follow-up, hemoglobin changed by 0.55 g/dL (95% CI, 0.49 to 0.61 g/dL; P < .001) in the SGLT2 inhibitor group, and -0.08 g/dL (95% CI, -0.13 to -0.03 g/dL; P = .003) in the control group (difference, 0.60 g/dL; 95% CI, 0.55 to 0.66 g/dL; P < .001). From baseline to follow-up, hematocrit changed by 2.17% (95% CI, 1.95% to 2.39%; P < .001) in the SGLT2 inhibitor group and -0.13% (95% CI, -0.28% to 0.01%; P = .07) in the control group (difference, 2.36%; 95% CI, 2.19% to 2.52%; P < .001). Of the 6 secondary outcomes, 4 showed significant associations with SGLT2 inhibitors, including increased EPO (2.35 mIU/mL in SGLT2 inhibitor group vs 0.04 mIU/mL in control group; difference, 2.11 mIU/mL [95% CI, 0.5-3.71 mIU/mL]; P = .01) and decreased transferrin saturation (-2.2% in SGLT2 inhibitor group vs -0.10% in control group; difference, -0.84% [95% CI, -1.21% to -0.47%]; P < .001).<h4>Conclusions and relevance</h4>In a meta-analysis of randomized clinical trials, compared with control, SGLT2 inhibitor use was associated with small statistically significant increases in hemoglobin and hematocrit levels. The clinical significance of these findings is unclear.

### [Tarlatamab-Durvalumab Maintenance for Extensive-Stage Small-Cell Lung Cancer.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2609482)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-24` · freshness `fresh`

<h4>Background</h4>Patients with extensive-stage small-cell lung cancer have poor outcomes, with a median overall survival of approximately 12 to 13 months. Tarlatamab (a bispecific T-cell engager targeting delta-like ligand 3 [DLL3] and CD3) was previously shown to improve survival as second-line therapy. Whether adding tarlatamab to durvalumab (an anti-programmed death ligand 1 agent) after initial chemoimmunotherapy could significantly improve survival in the context of maintenance therapy after first remission has been unclear.<h4>Methods</h4>In this phase 3, international, open-label, randomized trial, we compared tarlatamab-durvalumab with durvalumab as first-line maintenance therapy in patients with extensive-stage small-cell lung cancer without progression after initial chemoimmunotherapy. Overall survival and progression-free survival were assessed from randomization at the start of maintenance therapy as the primary and key secondary end points, respectively. Results of the prespecified interim analysis (data-cutoff date, July 7, 2026) are reported here.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 563 patients were randomly assigned to receive tarlatamab-durvalumab (282 patients) or durvalumab (281 patients). At a median follow-up of 15.9 months in the tarlatamab-durvalumab group and 16.1 months in the durvalumab group, the estimated median overall survival was 21.1 months (95% confidence interval [CI], 20.8 to could not be estimated) with tarlatamab-durvalumab and 12.7 months (95% CI, 11.2 to 14.8) with durvalumab (hazard ratio for death, 0.42; 95% CI, 0.32 to 0.54; P<0.001). The median progression-free survival was 7.4 months (95% CI, 5.6 to 9.1) with tarlatamab-durvalumab and 1.9 months (95% CI, 1.9 to 2.0) with durvalumab (hazard ratio for disease progression or death, 0.34; 95% CI, 0.28 to 0.42; P<0.001). Adverse events of grade 3 or higher occurred in 46.8% of the patients who received tarlatamab-durvalumab and in 40.6% of those who received durvalumab alone. Cytokine release syndrome, mainly of grade 1 or 2, was seen in nearly half the patients receiving tarlatamab-durvalumab.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Tarlatamab-durvalumab maintenance therapy resulted in significantly longer overall survival and progression-free survival than durvalumab alone among patients with extensive-stage small-cell lung cancer. (Funded by Amgen and AstraZeneca; DeLLphi-305 ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT06211036.).

### [Divarasib as Compared with Sotorasib or Adagrasib in <i>KRAS</i> G12C-Mutated NSCLC.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2605703)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42929675`

<h4>Background</h4>Divarasib, a next-generation, oral KRAS G12C inhibitor, is more potent and selective in vitro than the first-generation KRAS G12C inhibitors sotorasib and adagrasib.<h4>Methods</h4>In this phase 3, international, open-label trial, patients with previously treated <i>KRAS</i> G12C-mutated advanced or metastatic non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) were randomly assigned to receive divarasib orally at a dose of 400 mg once daily or control treatment (sotorasib orally at a dose of 960 mg once daily or adagrasib orally at a dose of 600 mg twice daily) until the occurrence of disease progression, unacceptable adverse events, loss of clinical benefit, withdrawal of consent, death, or trial termination. The primary end point was progression-free survival as assessed by blinded independent central review. Secondary end points included overall survival, confirmed overall response, duration of response, patient-reported outcomes, and safety.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 338 patients received divarasib (167 patients) or control treatment (171 patients; 156 received sotorasib and 15 received adagrasib). The median follow-up was 14.3 months. The median progression-free survival was 9.8 months with divarasib and 5.7 months with control treatment (hazard ratio for disease progression or death, 0.58; 95% confidence interval [CI], 0.44 to 0.76; P<0.001). The median overall survival was 17.7 months with divarasib and 12.0 months with control treatment (hazard ratio for death, 0.56; 95% CI, 0.41 to 0.78; P<0.001). Of the 328 patients who could be evaluated for response, a confirmed response occurred in 58.4% in the divarasib group and in 32.3% in the control group. The median duration of response was 11.9 months with divarasib and 6.9 months with control treatment. Fewer grade 3 or 4 treatment-related adverse events were noted with divarasib (24.6%) than with control treatment (34.5%).<h4>Conclusions</h4>Among patients with previously treated <i>KRAS</i> G12C-mutated advanced or metastatic NSCLC, divarasib led to significantly longer progression-free and overall survival than control treatment. (Supported by F. Hoffmann-La Roche; Krascendo 1 ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT06497556.).

### [Osimertinib-Savolitinib in Advanced NSCLC after Progression during EGFR-TKI Therapy.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2611335)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42929693`

<h4>Background</h4>Savolitinib is an oral, potent, highly selective MET-tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI). Combination therapy with savolitinib and osimertinib, an epidermal growth factor receptor (EGFR)-TKI, has shown activity in patients with <i>EGFR</i>-mutated, advanced non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) with MET overexpression, <i>MET</i> amplification, or both, after progression during EGFR-TKI therapy.<h4>Methods</h4>In this international, phase 3, open-label trial, we randomly assigned, in a 1:1 ratio, patients with <i>EGFR</i>-mutated, locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with MET overexpression (staining intensity of 3+ for MET in ≥90% of tumor cells on immunohistochemical analysis), <i>MET</i> amplification (≥10 gene copies on fluorescence in situ hybridization), or both whose disease had progressed during osimertinib therapy to receive osimertinib (at a dose of 80 mg once daily) plus savolitinib (300 mg twice daily) or intravenous platinum-pemetrexed. The primary end point was progression-free survival, as assessed by blinded independent central review. Secondary end points included overall survival and safety.<h4>Results</h4>Of the 1746 patients screened, 338 were randomly assigned to receive osimertinib-savolitinib (171 patients) or platinum-pemetrexed (167 patients). Osimertinib-savolitinib led to significantly longer progression-free survival than platinum-pemetrexed (hazard ratio for disease progression or death, 0.55; 95% confidence interval [CI], 0.42 to 0.72; P<0.001), as well as significantly longer overall survival (hazard ratio for death, 0.68; 95% CI, 0.50 to 0.91; P = 0.01; final analysis). The Kaplan-Meier estimate of 12-month progression-free survival was 36% in the osimertinib-savolitinib group and 10% in the platinum-pemetrexed group, and the estimate of 12-month overall survival was 70% and 53%, respectively. The most frequently reported adverse events that occurred during treatment in the osimertinib-savolitinib group were peripheral edema (in 49% of the patients), anemia (in 26%), and diarrhea (in 26%). Grade 3 or higher adverse events during treatment were reported in 64% of the patients in the osimertinib-savolitinib group and in 63% of those in the platinum-pemetrexed group.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Osimertinib-savolitinib led to significantly longer progression-free and overall survival than platinum-pemetrexed among patients with <i>EGFR</i>-mutated, locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC with MET overexpression, <i>MET</i> amplification, or both that had progressed during osimertinib therapy (Funded by AstraZeneca; SAFFRON ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT05261399.).

### [Sacituzumab Tirumotecan plus Pembrolizumab in PD-L1-Negative Advanced NSCLC.](https://doi.org/10.1056/nejmoa2611280)

- Source: `nejm_abstracts` · published `2026-10-25` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42929694`

<h4>Background</h4>Pembrolizumab plus chemotherapy with a platinum-based agent and pemetrexed is a widely used standard first-line treatment option for patients with programmed death ligand 1 (PD-L1)-negative advanced nonsquamous non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) without targetable genomic alterations, but outcomes remain suboptimal.<h4>Methods</h4>In this phase 3, open-label, randomized trial, we evaluated sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab in patients with previously untreated locally advanced or metastatic nonsquamous NSCLC without targetable genomic alterations and a PD-L1 tumor proportion score of less than 1%. Patients were randomly assigned in a 1:1 ratio to receive sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab or chemotherapy plus pembrolizumab. The primary end point was progression-free survival as assessed by blinded independent central review. Overall survival was a secondary end point. This report presents the results of the prespecified interim analysis.<h4>Results</h4>A total of 455 patients were randomly assigned to receive sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab (229 patients) or chemotherapy plus pembrolizumab (226 patients). After a median follow-up of 9.7 months, the median progression-free survival was significantly longer with sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab than with chemotherapy plus pembrolizumab (15.0 months vs. 5.7 months; hazard ratio for disease progression or death, 0.49; 95% confidence interval [CI], 0.36 to 0.65; P<0.001). An objective response occurred in 50.2% and 47.3% of the patients, respectively, and the median duration of response was 13.6 months and 6.8 months. Overall survival data were immature; the hazard ratio for death was 0.58 (95% CI, 0.38 to 0.88). Adverse events of grade 3 or higher that occurred after the start of treatment were reported in 47.6% of the patients assigned to receive sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab and in 60.6% of those assigned to receive chemotherapy plus pembrolizumab. Stomatitis was common with sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab, but the events were predominantly grade 1 or 2.<h4>Conclusions</h4>Among patients with PD-L1-negative advanced nonsquamous NSCLC without targetable genomic alterations, first-line treatment with sacituzumab tirumotecan plus pembrolizumab led to significantly longer progression-free survival than chemotherapy plus pembrolizumab. (Funded by Sichuan Kelun-Biotech Biopharmaceutical; OptiTROP-Lung06 ClinicalTrials.gov number, NCT06711900.).

### [Advances in the management of renal cell carcinoma.](https://doi.org/10.1136/bmj-2026-100785)

- Source: `bmj_abstracts` · published `2026-10-26` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42933810`

Renal cell carcinoma (RCC) is the most common form of kidney cancer, with 435 000 patients newly diagnosed worldwide each year. In addition to morphology and histology, molecular subtyping has advanced the diagnostic classification of RCC. Contemporary diagnosis relies on cross sectional imaging in the form of computed tomography or magnetic resonance imaging and selective biopsy. For localised disease, nephrectomy remains the standard of care, with partial nephrectomy being an option for smaller tumours. Ablation or stereotactic radiation is appropriate for selected patients. Adjuvant pembrolizumab provides improved disease-free and overall survival in high risk clear cell RCC. In metastatic disease, the introduction of immune checkpoint inhibitor based combinations has extended overall survival, providing a durable response in some patients. Despite these advances, primary and secondary resistance, along with the lack of predictive and prognostic biomarkers, remain a challenge.

### [First-line pembrolizumab versus carboplatin plus paclitaxel in participants with mismatch repair-deficient advanced or recurrent endometrial cancer (KEYNOTE-C93/GOG-3064/ENGOT-en15): interim analysis of a multicentre, open-label, randomised, active-comparator controlled, phase 3 trial.](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0140-6736(26)02095-7)

- Source: `lancet_abstracts` · published `2026-10-26` · freshness `fresh`
- Reading value: `pending` · status: `failed`
- Event: `seen` · identity: `pmid:42933458`

<h4>Background</h4>A need exists for first-line chemotherapy-free options in advanced or recurrent endometrial cancer. We therefore assessed the efficacy, quality of life, and safety of first-line pembrolizumab versus carboplatin plus paclitaxel in mismatch repair-deficient (dMMR) advanced or recurrent endometrial cancer.<h4>Methods</h4>The KEYNOTE-C93/GOG-3064/ENGOT-en15 multicentre, open-label, randomised, active-comparator controlled, phase 3 trial was conducted at 122 centres in 24 countries in the Americas, Asia, Europe, and Oceania. Adult participants (≥18 years) with inoperable, stage III or IV, persistent, advanced, or recurrent dMMR endometrial cancer were randomly assigned 1:1 to intravenous pembrolizumab 400 mg every 6 weeks for up to 18 cycles or intravenous carboplatin area under the curve 5 mg/mL or 6 mg/mL per min plus paclitaxel 175 mg/m<sup>2</sup> on day 1 of each 3-week cycle for six cycles. Randomisation was stratified by disease status, histology, and previous receipt of (neo)adjuvant chemotherapy. Primary endpoints were progression-free survival per RECIST version 1.1, by blinded independent central review, and overall survival. This trial is registered at ClinicalTrials.gov, NCT05173987, and is ongoing.<h4>Findings</h4>Between Feb 22, 2022, and Dec 12, 2023, 299 participants were assigned to pembrolizumab (n=149) or carboplatin plus paclitaxel (n=150). At the cutoff for the prespecified interim analysis in the intention-to-treat population (April 24, 2026), median progression-free survival was 38·2 months (95% CI 17·9 months-not reached) in the pembrolizumab group versus 8·4 months (6·8-10·4) in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group (hazard ratio [HR] 0·57, 95% CI 0·42-0·79; p=0·0002). Estimated progression-free survival was 75% (95% CI 67-81) versus 76% (68-82) at 6 months, 67% (59-74) versus 40% (32-48) at 12 months, and 53% (44-61) versus 30% (22-38) at 36 months. The prespecified statistical significance criterion for overall survival was not met at the interim analysis (medians not reached; HR 0·77, 95% CI 0·52-1·15; p=0·10). Grade 3 or higher treatment-related adverse events occurred in 27 (18%) of 149 participants in the pembrolizumab group and 73 (51%) of 144 in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group. Treatment-related adverse events of any grade that occurred in at least 25% of participants in either group included alopecia (three [2%] of 149 in the pembrolizumab group, 72 [50%] of 144 in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group), anaemia (seven [5%], 71 [49%]), nausea (seven [5%], 41 [28%]), decreased neutrophil count (five [3%], 40 [28%]), peripheral neuropathy (none, 38 [26%]), and fatigue (20 [13%], 37 [26%]). Treatment-related adverse events led to treatment discontinuation in 14 (9%) of 149 participants in the pembrolizumab group and 21 (15%) of 144 participants in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group; they resulted in death in two participants in the pembrolizumab group (hepatic failure and bacterial meningitis [n=1 each]) and one participant in the carboplatin plus paclitaxel group (pulmonary embolism).<h4>Interpretation</h4>First-line pembrolizumab showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in progression-free survival, with lower frequency of overall and grade 3 or above treatment-related adverse events. The study did not compare pembrolizumab monotherapy with the current standard of care (immunotherapy plus chemotherapy).<h4>Funding</h4>Merck Sharp & Dohme, a subsidiary of Merck & Co.
